<div class="field field--name-field-title field--type-string field--label-hidden field__item"><a href="/document/28471-document-52-davies-diary-entry-july-29-1945" hreflang="en"><strong class=" wrapped-title raw-title">Davies Diary entry for July 29, 1945</strong></a></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-date field--type-datetime field--label-hidden field__item"><time datetime="1945-07-29T12:00:00Z" class="datetime">Jul 29, 1945</time>

This diary entry has figured in the argument that Byrnes believed that the atomic bomb gave the United States a significant advantage in negotiations with the Soviet Union. Plainly Davies thought otherwise.<a name="_ednref50"></a><a href="#_edn50">[50]</a></p>

    <h3 id="viii-debates-among-the-japanese-ndash-late-julyearly-august-1945" class="entity-list-title"><a href="#_tcref7" name="_tc7">VIII. Debates among the Japanese &ndash; Late July/Early August 1945</a></h3>

<div class="field field--name-field-documents field--type-entity-reference field--label-hidden field__items">
              <div class="field__item"><article class="media media--type-document media--view-mode-document-list">

<div class="row">
              <div class="col-auto thumbnail-column">

<div class="field field--name-thumbnail field--type-image field--label-hidden field__item">  <img loading="lazy" src="/sites/default/files/styles/thumbnail/public/documents/rdl5ia-iggzy/53.pdf.jpeg?itok=RhlBITdb" alt="53" typeof="foaf:Image" class="image-style-thumbnail" />

</div>

</div>

<div class="col fields-column">

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-section field--type-text-long field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Document 53</p></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-title field--type-string field--label-hidden field__item"><a href="/document/28472-document-53-magic-diplomatic-summary-war-department-office-assistant-chief-staff-g-2" hreflang="en"><strong class=" wrapped-title raw-title">“Magic” – Diplomatic Summary, War Department, Office of Assistant Chief of Staff, G-2, No. 1221- July 29, 1945, Top Secret Ultra</strong></a></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-date field--type-datetime field--label-hidden field__item"><time datetime="1945-07-29T12:00:00Z" class="datetime">Jul 29, 1945</time>
</div>tra</strong></a></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-date field--type-datetime field--label-hidden field__item"><time datetime="1945-07-29T12:00:00Z" class="datetime">Jul 29, 1945</time>
</div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-source field--type-text-long field--label-inline">
    <div class="field__label">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p>Record Group 457, Records of the National Security Agency/Central Security Service, “Magic” Diplomatic Summaries 1942-1945, box 18.</p>
</div>
          </div><div class="field__item"><p>Record Group 457, Records of the National Security Agency/Central Security Service, “Magic” Diplomatic Summaries 1942-1945, box 18.</p>
</div>
          </div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-description field--type-text-with-summary field--label-hidden field__item"><p>In the <a href="http://rbscarchives.library.ubc.ca/uploads/r/university-of-british-columbia-library-rare-books-and-special-collections/e/c/ecae1ed788d4c9e606fdf31329904e888a0583f89a38a9c5cc212614edae5799/9bed4ea1-519a-4a58-a5ed-f59a32d786c8-rbsc_arc_1135_30_15_001.pdf">Potsdam Declaration</a> the governments of China, Great Britain, and the United States) demanded the “unconditional surrender of all Japanese armed forces. “The alternative is prompt and utter destruction.” The next day, in response to questions from journalists about the government’s reaction to the ultimatum, Prime Minister Suzuki apparently said that “We can only ignore [<em>mokusatsu</em>] it. We will do our utmost to complete the war to the bitter end.” That, Bix argues, represents a “missed opportunity” to end the war and spare the Japanese from continued U.S. aerial attacks.<a name="_ednref51"></a><a href="#_edn51">[51]</a> Togo’s private position was more nuanced than Suzuki’s; he told Sato that “we are adopting a policy of careful study.” That Stalin had not signed the declaration (Truman and Churchill did not ask him to) led to questions about the Soviet attitude. Togo asked Sato to try to meet with Soviet Foreign Minister Molotov as soon as possible to “sound out the Russian attitude” on the declaration as well as Japan’s end-the-war initiative. Sato cabled Togo earlier that he saw no point in approaching the Soviets on ending the war until Tokyo had “concrete proposals.” “Any aid from the Soviets has now become extremely doubtful.”</p>
</div>

</div>ed Togo earlier that he saw no point in approaching the Soviets on ending the war until Tokyo had “concrete proposals.” “Any aid from the Soviets has now become extremely doubtful.”</p>
</div>

</div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-section field--type-text-long field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Document 54</p></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-title field--type-string field--label-hidden field__item"><a href="/document/28473-document-54-magic-diplomatic-summary-war-department-office-assistant-chief-staff-g-2" hreflang="en"><strong class=" wrapped-title raw-title">“Magic” – Diplomatic Summary, War Department, Office of Assistant Chief of Staff, G-2, No. 1222 – July 30, 1945, Top Secret Ultra</strong></a></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-date field--type-datetime field--label-hidden field__item"><time datetime="1945-07-30T12:00:00Z" class="datetime">Jul 30, 1945</time>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-source field--type-text-long field--label-inline">
    <div class="field__label">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p>Record Group 457, Records of the National Security Agency/Central Security Service, “Magic” Diplomatic Summaries 1942-1945, box 18.</p>
</div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-description field--type-text-with-summary field--label-hidden field__item"><p>This report included an intercept of a message from Sato reporting that it was impossible to see Molotov and that unless the Togo had a “concrete and definite plan for terminating the war” he saw no point in attempting to meet with him.</p>

<div class="col fields-column">

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-section field--type-text-long field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Document 55</p></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-title field--type-string field--label-hidden field__item"><a href="/document/28474-document-55-magic-diplomatic-summary-war-department-office-assistant-chief-staff-g-2" hreflang="en"><strong class=" wrapped-title raw-title">“Magic” – Diplomatic Summary, War Department, Office of Assistant Chief of Staff, G-2, No. 1225 – August 2, 1945, Top Secret Ultra</strong></a></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-date field--type-datetime field--label-hidden field__item"><time datetime="1945-08-02T12:00:00Z" class="datetime">Aug 2, 1945</time>
</div>ltra</strong></a></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-date field--type-datetime field--label-hidden field__item"><time datetime="1945-08-02T12:00:00Z" class="datetime">Aug 2, 1945</time>
</div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-source field--type-text-long field--label-inline">
    <div class="field__label">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p>Record Group 457, Records of the National Security Agency/Central Security Service, “Magic” Diplomatic Summaries 1942-1945, box 18.</p>
</div>
          </div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-description field--type-text-with-summary field--label-hidden field__item"><p>An intercepted message from Togo to Sato showed that Tokyo remained interested in securing Moscow’s good office but that it “is difficult to decide on concrete peace conditions here at home all at once.” “[W]e are exerting ourselves to collect the views of all quarters on the matter of concrete terms.” Barton Bernstein, Richard Frank, and Tsuyoshi Hasegawa, among others, have argued that the “Magic” intercepts from the end of July and early August show that the Japanese were far from ready to surrender. According to Herbert Bix, for months Hirohito had believed that the “outlook for a negotiated peace could be improved if Japan fought and won one last decisive battle,” thus, he delayed surrender, continuing to “procrastinate until the bomb was dropped and the Soviets attacked.”<a name="_ednref52"></a><a href="#_edn52">[52]</a></p>
</div>

</div>

</div>
  </article>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-section field--type-text-long field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Document 57</p></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-title field--type-string field--label-hidden field__item"><a href="/document/28476-document-57-walter-brown-meeting-notes-august-3-1945" hreflang="en"><strong class=" wrapped-title raw-title">Walter Brown Meeting Notes, August 3, 1945</strong></a></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-date field--type-datetime field--label-hidden field__item"><time datetime="1945-08-03T12:00:00Z" class="datetime">Aug 3, 1945</time>
</div>1945</strong></a></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-date field--type-datetime field--label-hidden field__item"><time datetime="1945-08-03T12:00:00Z" class="datetime">Aug 3, 1945</time>
</div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-source field--type-text-long field--label-inline">
    <div class="field__label">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p>Clemson University Libraries, Special Collections, Clemson, SC; Mss 243, Walter J. Brown Papers, box 10, folder 12, Byrnes, James F.: Potsdam, Minutes, July-August 1945</p>
</div>
          </div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-description field--type-text-with-summary field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Historians have used this item in the papers of Byrne’s aide, Walter Brown, to make a variety of points. Richard Frank sees this brief discussion of Japan’s interest in Soviet diplomatic assistance as crucial evidence that Admiral Leahy had been sharing “MAGIC” information with President Truman. He also points out that Truman and his colleagues had no idea what was behind Japanese peace moves, only that Suzuki had declared that he would “ignore” the Potsdam Declaration. Alperovitz, however, treats it as additional evidence that “strongly suggests” that Truman saw alternatives to using the bomb.<a name="_ednref53"></a><a href="#_edn53">[53]</a></p>
</div>

</div>

<div class="field field--name-field-title field--type-string field--label-hidden field__item"><a href="/document/28477-document-58-magic-far-east-summary-war-department-office-assistant-chief-staff-g-2" hreflang="en"><strong class=" wrapped-title raw-title">“Magic” – Far East Summary, War Department, Office of Assistant Chief of Staff, G-2, no. 502, 4 August 1945</strong></a></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-date field--type-datetime field--label-hidden field__item"><time datetime="1945-08-04T12:00:00Z" class="datetime">Aug 4, 1945</time>
</div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-source field--type-text-long field--label-inline">
    <div class="field__label">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p>RG 457, Summaries of Intercepted Japanese Messages (“Magic” Far East Summary, March 20, 1942 – October 2, 1945), box 7, SRS 491-547</p>
</div>
          </div><div class="field__item"><p>RG 457, Summaries of Intercepted Japanese Messages (“Magic” Far East Summary, March 20, 1942 – October 2, 1945), box 7, SRS 491-547</p>
</div>
          </div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-description field--type-text-with-summary field--label-hidden field__item"><p>This “Far East Summary” included reports on the Japanese Army’s plans to disperse fuel stocks to reduce vulnerability to bombing attacks, the text of a directive by the commander of naval forces on “Operation Homeland,” the preparations and planning to repel a U.S. invasion of Honshu, and the specific identification of army divisions located in, or moving into, Kyushu. Both Richard Frank and Barton Bernstein have used intelligence reporting and analysis of the major buildup of Japanese forces on southern Kyushu to argue that U.S. military planners were so concerned about this development that by early August 1945 they were reconsidering their invasion plans.<a name="_ednref54"></a><a href="#_edn54">[54]</a></p>
</div>

</div>

  <div class="entity-list">
    <h3 id="ix-the-execution-order-nbsp-documents-60a-d-these-messages-convey-the-process-of-creating-and-transmitting-the-execution-order-to-bomb-hiroshima-possibly-not-wanting-to-take-responsibility-for-the-first-use-of-nuclear-weapons-army-air-force-commanders-sought-formal-authorization-from-chief-of-staff-marshall-who-was-then-in-potsdam" class="entity-list-title"><a href="#_tcref8" name="_tc8">IX. The Execution Order</a> <p>&nbsp;</p> <p><b>Documents 60A-D: These messages convey the process of creating and transmitting the execution order to bomb Hiroshima.  Possibly not wanting to take responsibility for the first use of nuclear weapons, Army Air Force commanders sought formal authorization from Chief of Staff Marshall who was then in Potsdam</b></p></h3>

<div class="field field--name-field-documents field--type-entity-reference field--label-hidden field__items">
              <div class="field__item"><article class="media media--type-document media--view-mode-document-list">

<div class="row">
              <div class="col-auto thumbnail-column">

<div class="field field--name-thumbnail field--type-image field--label-hidden field__item">  <img loading="lazy" src="/sites/default/files/styles/thumbnail/public/documents/rdl5gv-i24g8/60a.pdf.jpeg?itok=lA_XtINL" alt="60a" typeof="foaf:Image" class="image-style-thumbnail" />

</div>

</div>

<div class="col fields-column">

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-section field--type-text-long field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Document 60A</p>
</div></div>

</div>

<div class="col fields-column">

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-section field--type-text-long field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Document 60A</p>
</div>

<div class="field field--name-field-title field--type-string field--label-hidden field__item"><a href="/document/28479-document-60a-cable-victory-213-marshall-handy-july-22-1945-top-secret" hreflang="en"><strong class=" wrapped-title raw-title">Cable VICTORY 213 from Marshall to Handy, July 22, 1945, Top Secret</strong></a></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-date field--type-datetime field--label-hidden field__item"><time datetime="1945-07-22T12:00:00Z" class="datetime">Jul 22, 1945</time>
</div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-source field--type-text-long field--label-inline">
    <div class="field__label">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p>RG 77, MED Records, Top Secret Documents, Files no. 5b and 5e (copies from microfilm)</p>
</div>
          </div>>
    <div class="field__label">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p>RG 77, MED Records, Top Secret Documents, Files no. 5b and 5e (copies from microfilm)</p>
</div>
          </div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-description field--type-text-with-summary field--label-hidden field__item"><p>These messages convey the process of creating and transmitting the execution order to bomb Hiroshima. &nbsp;Possibly not wanting to take responsibility for the first use of nuclear weapons, Army Air Force commanders sought formal authorization from Chief of Staff Marshall who was then in Potsdam.<a name="_ednref55"></a><a href="#_edn55">[55]</a> On 22 July Marshall asked Deputy Chief of Staff Thomas Handy to prepare a draft; General Groves wrote one which went to Potsdam for Marshall’s approval. Colonel John Stone, an assistant to commanding General of the Army Air Forces Henry H. “Hap” Arnold, had just returned from Potsdam and updated his boss on the plans as they had developed. On 25 July Marshall informed Handy that Secretary of War Stimson had approved the text; that same day, Handy signed off on a directive which ordered the use of atomic weapons on Japan, with the first weapon assigned to one of four possible targets—Hiroshima, Kokura, Niigata, or Nagasaki. “Additional bombs will be delivery on the [targets] as soon as made ready by the project staff.”</p>
</div>

</div>

</div>
  </article>
</div>

              <div class="field__item"><article class="media media--type-document media--view-mode-document-list">

<div class="row">
              <div class="col-auto thumbnail-column">

<div class="field field--name-thumbnail field--type-image field--label-hidden field__item">  <img loading="lazy" src="/sites/default/files/styles/thumbnail/public/documents/61.pdf.jpeg?itok=wi6xRX0J" alt="61" typeof="foaf:Image" class="image-style-thumbnail" />ld--label-hidden field__item">  <img loading="lazy" src="/sites/default/files/styles/thumbnail/public/documents/61.pdf.jpeg?itok=wi6xRX0J" alt="61" typeof="foaf:Image" class="image-style-thumbnail" />

<div class="col fields-column">

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-section field--type-text-long field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Document 61</p>
</div>

<div class="field field--name-field-title field--type-string field--label-hidden field__item"><a href="/document/28484-document-61-memorandum-major-general-l-r-groves-chief-staff-july-30-1945-top-secret" hreflang="en"><strong class=" wrapped-title raw-title">Memorandum from Major General L. R. Groves to Chief of Staff, July 30, 1945, Top Secret, Sanitized Copy</strong></a></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-date field--type-datetime field--label-hidden field__item"><time datetime="1945-07-30T12:00:00Z" class="datetime">Jul 30, 1945</time>
</div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-source field--type-text-long field--label-inline">
    <div class="field__label">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p>RG 77, MED Records, Top Secret Documents, File no. 5</p>
</div>
          </div>pe-text-long field--label-inline">
    <div class="field__label">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p>RG 77, MED Records, Top Secret Documents, File no. 5</p>
</div>
          </div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-description field--type-text-with-summary field--label-hidden field__item"><p>With more information on the Alamogordo test available, Groves provided Marshall with detail on the destructive power of atomic weapons. Barton J. Bernstein has observed that Groves’ recommendation that troops could move into the “immediate explosion area” within a half hour demonstrates the prevalent lack of top-level knowledge of the dangers of nuclear weapons effects.<a name="_ednref56"></a><a href="#_edn56">[56]</a> Groves also provided the schedule for the delivery of the weapons: the components of the gun-type bomb to be used on Hiroshima had arrived on Tinian, while the parts of the second weapon to be dropped were leaving San Francisco. By the end of November over ten weapons would be available, presumably in the event the war had continued.</p>
</div>

</div>

</div>
  </article>
</div>

    <h3 id="documents-62a-c-weather-delays" class="entity-list-title"><p><b>Documents 62A-C: Weather delays</b></p></h3>

<div class="field field--name-field-documents field--type-entity-reference field--label-hidden field__items">

<div class="col fields-column">

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-section field--type-text-long field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Document 62A</p>
</div>

<div class="field field--name-field-title field--type-string field--label-hidden field__item"><a href="/document/28485-document-62a-cg-313th-bomb-wing-tinian-cable-apcom-5112-war-department-august-3-1945" hreflang="en"><strong class=" wrapped-title raw-title">CG 313th Bomb Wing, Tinian cable APCOM 5112 to War Department, August 3, 1945, Top Secret</strong></a></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-date field--type-datetime field--label-hidden field__item"><time datetime="1945-08-03T12:00:00Z" class="datetime">Aug 3, 1945</time>
</div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-source field--type-text-long field--label-inline">
    <div class="field__label">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p>RG 77, Tinian Files, April-December 1945, box 21 (copies courtesy of Barton Bernstein)</p>
</div>
          </div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-description field--type-text-with-summary field--label-hidden field__item">
<p>The Hiroshima “operation” was originally slated to begin in early August depending on local conditions. As these cables indicate, reports of unfavorable weather delayed the plan. The second cable on 4 August shows that the schedule advanced to late in the evening of 5 August. The handwritten transcriptions are on the original archival copies.</p>
</div>

</div>

  <div class="entity-list">
    <h3 id="x-the-first-nuclear-strikes-and-their-impact" class="entity-list-title"><a href="#_tcref9" name="_tc9">X. The First Nuclear Strikes and their Impact</a></h3>v>
  <div class="entity-list">
    <h3 id="x-the-first-nuclear-strikes-and-their-impact" class="entity-list-title"><a href="#_tcref9" name="_tc9">X. The First Nuclear Strikes and their Impact</a></h3>

<div class="field field--name-field-documents field--type-entity-reference field--label-hidden field__items">
              <div class="field__item"><article class="media media--type-document media--view-mode-document-list">

<div class="row">
              <div class="col-auto thumbnail-column">

<div class="field field--name-thumbnail field--type-image field--label-hidden field__item">  <img loading="lazy" src="/sites/default/files/styles/thumbnail/public/documents/rdl5gv-i24g8/63.pdf.jpeg?itok=DTIwghgT" alt="63" typeof="foaf:Image" class="image-style-thumbnail" />

</div>

</div>

<div class="col fields-column">

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-section field--type-text-long field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Document 63</p></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-title field--type-string field--label-hidden field__item"><a href="/document/28488-document-63-memorandum-general-l-r-groves-chief-staff-august-6-1945-top-secret" hreflang="en"><strong class=" wrapped-title raw-title">Memorandum from General L. R. Groves to the Chief of Staff, August 6, 1945, Top Secret</strong></a></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-date field--type-datetime field--label-hidden field__item"><time datetime="1945-08-06T12:00:00Z" class="datetime">Aug 6, 1945</time>
</div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-source field--type-text-long field--label-inline">
    <div class="field__label">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p>RG 77, MED Records, Top Secret Documents, File no. 5b (copy from microfilm)</p>
</div>
          </div>el-inline">
    <div class="field__label">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p>RG 77, MED Records, Top Secret Documents, File no. 5b (copy from microfilm)</p>
</div>
          </div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-description field--type-text-with-summary field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Two days after the bombing of Hiroshima, Groves provided Chief of Staff Marshall with a report which included messages from Captain William S. Parsons and others about the impact of the detonation which, through prompt radiation effects, fire storms, and blast effects, immediately killed at least 70,000, with many dying later from radiation sickness and other causes.<a name="_ednref57"></a><a href="#_edn57">[57]</a></p>

<p>How influential the atomic bombings of Hiroshima and later Nagasaki compared to the impact of the Soviet declaration of war were to the Japanese decision to surrender has been the subject of controversy among historians. Sadao Asada emphasizes the shock of the atomic bombs, while Herbert Bix has suggested that Hiroshima and the Soviet declaration of war made Hirohito and his court believe that failure to end the war could lead to the destruction of the imperial house. Frank and Hasegawa divide over the impact of the Soviet declaration of war, with Frank declaring that the Soviet intervention was “significant but not decisive” and Hasegawa arguing that the two atomic bombs “were not sufficient to change the direction of Japanese diplomacy. The Soviet invasion was.”<a name="_ednref58"></a><a href="#_edn58">[58]</a></p>
</div>

</div>

</div>
  </article>
</div>
              <div class="field__item"><article class="media media--type-document media--view-mode-document-list">

<div class="row">
              <div class="col-auto thumbnail-column">icle>
</div>
              <div class="field__item"><article class="media media--type-document media--view-mode-document-list">

<div class="row">
              <div class="col-auto thumbnail-column">

<div class="field field--name-thumbnail field--type-image field--label-hidden field__item">  <img loading="lazy" src="/sites/default/files/styles/thumbnail/public/documents/rdl5gv-i24g8/64.pdf.jpeg?itok=kXEBmkId" alt="64" typeof="foaf:Image" class="image-style-thumbnail" />

</div>

</div>

<div class="col fields-column">

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-section field--type-text-long field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Document 64</p></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-title field--type-string field--label-hidden field__item"><a href="/document/28489-document-64-walter-brown-diary-entry-6-august-1945" hreflang="en"><strong class=" wrapped-title raw-title">Walter Brown Diary Entry, 6 August 1945</strong></a></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-date field--type-datetime field--label-hidden field__item"><time datetime="1945-08-06T12:00:00Z" class="datetime">Aug 6, 1945</time>
</div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-source field--type-text-long field--label-inline">
    <div class="field__label">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p>Clemson University Libraries, Special Collections, Clemson, SC; Mss 243, Walter J. Brown Papers, box 68, folder 13, “Transcript/Draft B</p>
</div>
          </div><div class="field__item"><p>Clemson University Libraries, Special Collections, Clemson, SC; Mss 243, Walter J. Brown Papers, box 68, folder 13, “Transcript/Draft B</p>
</div>
          </div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-description field--type-text-with-summary field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Returning from the Potsdam Conference, sailing on the<em> U.S.S. Augusta</em>, Truman learned about the atomic bombing of Hiroshima and announced it twice, first to those in the wardroom (socializing/dining area for commissioned officers), and then to the sailors’ mess. Still unaware of radiation effects, Truman emphasized the explosive yield. Later, he met with Secretary of State Byrnes and they discussed the Manhattan Project’s secrecy and the huge expenditures. Truman, who had been chair of the Senate Special Committee to Investigate the National Defense Program, said that “only on the appeal of Secretary of War Stimson did he refrain and let the War Department continue with the experiment unmolested.”</p>
</div>

</div>

</div>
  </article>
</div>
              <div class="field__item"><article class="media media--type-document media--view-mode-document-list">

<div class="row">
              <div class="col-auto thumbnail-column">

<div class="field field--name-thumbnail field--type-image field--label-hidden field__item">  <img loading="lazy" src="/sites/default/files/styles/thumbnail/public/documents/rdl5gv-i24g8/65.pdf.jpeg?itok=kP2F0gnK" alt="65" typeof="foaf:Image" class="image-style-thumbnail" />

</div>

</div>

<div class="col fields-column">

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-section field--type-text-long field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Document 65</p></div>/>

</div>

</div>

<div class="col fields-column">

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-section field--type-text-long field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Document 65</p></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-title field--type-string field--label-hidden field__item"><a href="/document/28490-document-65-directive-supreme-command-headquarters-commander-chief-soviet-forces-th" hreflang="en"><strong class=" wrapped-title raw-title">Directive from the Supreme Command Headquarters to the Commander-in-Chief of the Soviet Forces in the Far East on the Start of Combat Operations, No. 11122, Signed by [Communist Party General Secretary Joseph] Stalin and [Chief of General Staff A.I.] Antonov, 7 August 1945 (translation by Anna Melyakova)</strong></a></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-date field--type-datetime field--label-hidden field__item"><time datetime="1945-08-07T12:00:00Z" class="datetime">Aug 7, 1945</time>
</div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-source field--type-text-long field--label-inline">
    <div class="field__label">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p>A. Zolotarev, ed., Sovetsko-Iaponskaia Voina 1945 Goda: Istoriia Voenno-Politicheskogo Protivoborstva Dvukh Derzhav v 30–40e Gody (Moscow: Terra, 1997 and 2000), Vol. 7 (1), 340-341.</p>
</div>
          </div>ev, ed., Sovetsko-Iaponskaia Voina 1945 Goda: Istoriia Voenno-Politicheskogo Protivoborstva Dvukh Derzhav v 30–40e Gody (Moscow: Terra, 1997 and 2000), Vol. 7 (1), 340-341.</p>
</div>
          </div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-description field--type-text-with-summary field--label-hidden field__item"><p>To keep his pledge at Yalta to enter the war against Japan and to secure the territorial concessions promised at the conference (e.g., Soviet annexation of the Kuriles and southern Sakhalin and a Soviet naval base at Port Arthur, etc.) Stalin considered various dates to schedule an attack. By early August he decided that 9-10 August 1945 would be the best dates for striking Japanese forces in Manchuria. In light of Japan’s efforts to seek Soviet mediation, Stalin wanted to enter the war quickly lest Tokyo reach a compromise peace with the Americans and the British at Moscow’s expense. But on 7 August, Stalin changed the instructions: the attack was to begin the next day. According to David Holloway, “it seems likely that the atomic bombing of Hiroshima the day before that impelled [Stalin] to speed up Soviet entry into the war” and “secure the gains promised at Yalta.”<a name="_ednref59"></a><a href="#_edn59">[59]</a></p>
</div>

</div>

</div>
  </article>
</div>

</div>

<div class="col fields-column">

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-section field--type-text-long field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Document 66</p></div>/>

</div>

</div>

<div class="col fields-column">

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-section field--type-text-long field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Document 66</p></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-title field--type-string field--label-hidden field__item"><a href="/document/28491-document-66-memorandum-conversation-atomic-bomb-august-7-1945" hreflang="en"><strong class=" wrapped-title raw-title">Memorandum of Conversation, “Atomic Bomb,” August 7, 1945</strong></a></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-date field--type-datetime field--label-hidden field__item"><time datetime="1945-08-07T12:00:00Z" class="datetime">Aug 7, 1945</time>
</div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-source field--type-text-long field--label-inline">
    <div class="field__label">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p>Library of Congress Manuscript Division, Papers of W. Averell Harriman, box 181, Chron File Aug 5-9, 1945.</p>
</div>
          </div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-description field--type-text-with-summary field--label-hidden field__item"><p>The Soviets already knew about the U.S. atomic project from espionage sources in the United States and Britain so Molotov’s comment to Ambassador Harriman about the secrecy surrounding the U.S. atomic project can be taken with a grain of salt, although the Soviets were probably unaware of specific plans for nuclear use.</p>
</div>

</div>

    <h3 id="documents-67a-bearly-high-level-reactions-to-the-hiroshima-bombing" class="entity-list-title"><p><b>Documents 67A-B: Early High-level Reactions to the Hiroshima Bombing</b></p></h3>list">
    <h3 id="documents-67a-bearly-high-level-reactions-to-the-hiroshima-bombing" class="entity-list-title"><p><b>Documents 67A-B: Early High-level Reactions to the Hiroshima Bombing</b></p></h3>

<div class="field field--name-field-documents field--type-entity-reference field--label-hidden field__items">
              <div class="field__item"><article class="media media--type-document media--view-mode-document-list">

<div class="row">
              <div class="col-auto thumbnail-column">

<div class="field field--name-thumbnail field--type-image field--label-hidden field__item">  <img loading="lazy" src="/sites/default/files/styles/thumbnail/public/documents/rdl5gv-i24g8/67a.pdf.jpeg?itok=ceItHlN_" alt="67a" typeof="foaf:Image" class="image-style-thumbnail" />

</div>

</div>

<div class="col fields-column">

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-section field--type-text-long field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Document 67A</p>
</div>

<div class="field field--name-field-title field--type-string field--label-hidden field__item"><a href="/document/28492-document-67a-cabinet-meeting-and-togos-meeting-emperor-august-7-8-1945" hreflang="en"><strong class=" wrapped-title raw-title">Cabinet Meeting and Togo's Meeting with the Emperor, August 7-8, 1945</strong></a></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-date field--type-datetime field--label-hidden field__item"><time datetime="1945-08-07T12:00:00Z" class="datetime">Aug 7, 1945</time>
</div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-source field--type-text-long field--label-inline">
    <div class="field__label">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p>Gaimusho (Ministry of Foreign Affairs) ed. Shusen Shiroku (The Historical Records of the End of the War), annotated by Jun Eto, volume 4, 57-60 [Excerpts] [Translation by Toshihiro Higuchi]</p></div>
          </div>ry of Foreign Affairs) ed. Shusen Shiroku (The Historical Records of the End of the War), annotated by Jun Eto, volume 4, 57-60 [Excerpts] [Translation by Toshihiro Higuchi]</p></div>
          </div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-description field--type-text-with-summary field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Excerpts from the Foreign Ministry's compilation about the end of the war show how news of the bombing reached Tokyo as well as how Foreign Minister's Togo initially reacted to reports about Hiroshima. When he learned of the atomic bombing from the Domei News Agency, Togo believed that it was time to give up and advised the cabinet that the atomic attack provided the occasion for Japan to surrender on the basis of the Potsdam Declaration. Togo could not persuade the cabinet, however, and the Army wanted to delay any decisions until it had learned what had happened to Hiroshima. When the Foreign Minister met with the Emperor, Hirohito agreed with him; he declared that the top priority was an early end to the war, although it would be acceptable to seek better surrender terms--probably U.S. acceptance of a figure-head emperor--if it did not interfere with that goal. In light of those instructions, Togo and Prime Minister Suzuki agreed that the Supreme War Council should meet the next day. <a href="#_edn59a" name="_ednref59a">[59A]</a></p></div>

</div>

</div>
  </article>
</div>
              <div class="field__item"><article class="media media--type-document media--view-mode-document-list">

</div>

<div class="col fields-column">default/files/styles/thumbnail/public/documents/rdl5gv-i24g8/67b.pdf.jpeg?itok=kPGfXXxi" alt="67b" typeof="foaf:Image" class="image-style-thumbnail" />

</div>

</div>

<div class="col fields-column">

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-section field--type-text-long field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Document 67B</p>
</div>

<div class="field field--name-field-title field--type-string field--label-hidden field__item"><a href="/document/28493-document-67b-admiral-takagi-diary-entry-wednesday-august-8-1945" hreflang="en"><strong class=" wrapped-title raw-title">Admiral Takagi Diary Entry for Wednesday, August 8 , 1945</strong></a></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-date field--type-datetime field--label-hidden field__item"><time datetime="1945-08-08T12:00:00Z" class="datetime">Aug 8, 1945</time>
</div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-source field--type-text-long field--label-inline">
    <div class="field__label">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p>Takashi Itoh, ed., Sokichi Takagi: Nikki to Joho [Sokichi Takagi: Diary and Documents] (Tokyo, Japan: Misuzu-Shobo, 2000), 923-924 [Translation by Hikaru Tajima]</p></div>
          </div>d__item"><p>Takashi Itoh, ed., Sokichi Takagi: Nikki to Joho [Sokichi Takagi: Diary and Documents] (Tokyo, Japan: Misuzu-Shobo, 2000), 923-924 [Translation by Hikaru Tajima]</p></div>
          </div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-description field--type-text-with-summary field--label-hidden field__item"><p>An entry from Admiral Takagi's diary for August 8 conveys more information on the mood in elite Japanese circles after Hiroshima, but before the Soviet declaration of war and the bombing of Nagasaki. Seeing the bombing of Hiroshima as a sign of a worsening situation at home, Takagi worried about further deterioration. Nevertheless, his diary suggests that military hard-liners were very much in charge and that Prime Minister Suzuki was talking tough against surrender, by evoking last ditch moments in Japanese history and warning of the danger that subordinate commanders might not obey surrender orders. The last remark aggravated Navy Minister Yonai who saw it as irresponsible. That the Soviets had made no responses to Sato's request for a meeting was understood as a bad sign; Yonai realized that the government had to prepare for the possibility that Moscow might not help. One of the visitors mentioned at the beginning of the entry was Iwao Yamazaki who became Minister of the Interior in the next cabinet.</p></div>

</div>

</div>
  </article>
</div>
          </div>

<div class="col fields-column">

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-section field--type-text-long field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Document 68</p></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-title field--type-string field--label-hidden field__item"><a href="/document/28494-document-68-navy-secretary-james-forrestal-president-truman-august-8-1945" hreflang="en"><strong class=" wrapped-title raw-title">Navy Secretary James Forrestal to President Truman, August 8, 1945</strong></a></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-date field--type-datetime field--label-hidden field__item"><time datetime="1945-08-08T12:00:00Z" class="datetime">Aug 8, 1945</time>
</div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-source field--type-text-long field--label-inline">
    <div class="field__label">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p>Naval Historical Center, Operational Archives, James Forrestal Diaries</p>
</div>
          </div>--label-inline">
    <div class="field__label">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p>Naval Historical Center, Operational Archives, James Forrestal Diaries</p>
</div>
          </div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-description field--type-text-with-summary field--label-hidden field__item"><p>General Douglas MacArthur had been slated as commander for military operations against Japan’s mainland, this letter to Truman from Forrestal shows that the latter believed that the matter was not settled. Richard Frank sees this as evidence of the uncertainty felt by senior officials about the situation in early August; Forrestal would not have been so “audacious” to take an action that could ignite a “political firestorm” if he “seriously thought the end of the war was near.”</p>
</div>

</div>

</div>

<div class="col fields-column">

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-section field--type-text-long field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Document 69</p></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-title field--type-string field--label-hidden field__item"><a href="/document/28495-document-69-memorandum-conversation-far-eastern-war-and-general-situation-august-8" hreflang="en"><strong class=" wrapped-title raw-title">Memorandum of Conversation, “Far Eastern War and General Situation,” August 8, 1945, Top Secret</strong></a></div>nd-general-situation-august-8" hreflang="en"><strong class=" wrapped-title raw-title">Memorandum of Conversation, “Far Eastern War and General Situation,” August 8, 1945, Top Secret</strong></a></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-date field--type-datetime field--label-hidden field__item"><time datetime="1945-08-08T12:00:00Z" class="datetime">Aug 8, 1945</time>
</div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-source field--type-text-long field--label-inline">
    <div class="field__label">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p>Library of Congress Manuscript Division, Papers of W. Averell Harriman, box 181, Chron File Aug 5-9, 1945</p>
</div>
          </div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-description field--type-text-with-summary field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Shortly after the Soviets declared war on Japan, in line with commitments made at the Yalta and Potsdam conferences, Ambassador Harriman met with Stalin, with George Kennan keeping the U.S. record of the meeting. After Stalin reviewed in considerable detail, Soviet military gains in the Far East, they discussed the possible impact of the atomic bombing on Japan’s position (Nagasaki had not yet been attacked) and the dangers and difficulty of an atomic weapons program. According to Hasegawa, this was an important, even “startling,” conversation: it showed that Stalin “took the atomic bomb seriously”; moreover, he disclosed that the Soviets were working on their own atomic program.<a name="_ednref60"></a><a href="#_edn60">[60]</a></p>
</div>

</div>

</div>
  </article>

<div class="field field--name-field-title field--type-string field--label-hidden field__item"><a href="/document/28424-document-70-entries-8-9-august-robert-p-meiklejohn-diary" hreflang="en"><strong class=" wrapped-title raw-title">Entries for 8-9 August, Robert P. Meiklejohn Diary</strong></a></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-date field--type-datetime field--label-hidden field__item"><time datetime="1945-08-08T12:00:00Z" class="datetime">Aug 8, 1945</time>
</div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-source field--type-text-long field--label-inline">
    <div class="field__label">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p>W.A. Harriman Papers, Library of Congress, box 211, Robert Pickens Meiklejohn World War II Diary At London and Moscow March 10, 1941-February 14, 1946, Volume II (Privately printed, 1980 [Printed from hand-written originals]) (Reproduced with permission)</p>
</div>
          </div>d War II Diary At London and Moscow March 10, 1941-February 14, 1946, Volume II (Privately printed, 1980 [Printed from hand-written originals]) (Reproduced with permission)</p>
</div>
          </div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-description field--type-text-with-summary field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Robert P. Meiklejohn, who worked as Ambassador W. A. Harriman’s administrative assistant at the U.S. Embassies in Moscow and London during and after World War II, kept a detailed diary of his experiences and observations. The entries for 8 and 9 August, prepared in light of the bombing of Hiroshima, include discussion of the British contribution to the Manhattan Project, Harriman (“his nibs’”) report on his meeting with Molotov about the Soviet declaration of war, and speculation about the impact of the bombing of Hiroshima on the Soviet decision. According to Meiklejohn, “None of us doubt that the atomic bomb speeded up the Soviets’ declaration of war.”</p>
</div>

</div>

</div>

<div class="col fields-column">

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-section field--type-text-long field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Document 71</p></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-title field--type-string field--label-hidden field__item"><a href="/document/28425-document-71-memorandum-conference-president-august-8-1945-1045-am" hreflang="en"><strong class=" wrapped-title raw-title">Memorandum of Conference with the President, August 8, 1945 at 10:45 AM</strong></a></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-date field--type-datetime field--label-hidden field__item"><time datetime="1945-08-08T12:00:00Z" class="datetime">Aug 8, 1945</time>
</div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-source field--type-text-long field--label-inline">
    <div class="field__label">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p>Henry Stimson Diary, Sterling Library, Yale University (microfilm at Library of Congress)</p>
</div>
          </div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-description field--type-text-with-summary field--label-hidden field__item"><p>At their first meeting after the dropping of the bomb on Hiroshima, Stimson briefed Truman on the scale of the destruction, with Truman recognizing the “terrible responsibility” that was on his shoulders. Consistent with his earlier attempts, Stimson encouraged Truman to find ways to expedite Japan’s surrender by using “kindness and tact” and not treating them in the same way as the Germans. They also discussed postwar legislation on the atom and the pending Henry D. Smyth report on the scientific work underlying the Manhattan project and postwar domestic controls of the atom.</p>
</div>

</div>

</div>

    <h3 id="documents-72a-c-the-attack-on-nagasaki" class="entity-list-title"><p><b>Documents 72A-C: The Attack on Nagasaki</b></p></h3></article>
</div>
          </div>

<div class="entity-list">
    <h3 id="documents-72a-c-the-attack-on-nagasaki" class="entity-list-title"><p><b>Documents 72A-C: The Attack on Nagasaki</b></p></h3>

<div class="field field--name-field-documents field--type-entity-reference field--label-hidden field__items">
              <div class="field__item"><article class="media media--type-document media--view-mode-document-list">

<div class="row">
              <div class="col-auto thumbnail-column">

<div class="field field--name-thumbnail field--type-image field--label-hidden field__item">  <img loading="lazy" src="/sites/default/files/styles/thumbnail/public/documents/rdl56b-89y7n/72a.pdf.jpeg?itok=LDXOogGA" alt="72a" typeof="foaf:Image" class="image-style-thumbnail" />

</div>

</div>

<div class="col fields-column">

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-section field--type-text-long field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Document 72A</p>
</div>

<div class="field field--name-field-title field--type-string field--label-hidden field__item"><a href="/document/28426-document-72a-cable-apcom-5445-general-farrell-oleary-groves-assistant-august-9-1945" hreflang="en"><strong class=" wrapped-title raw-title">Cable APCOM 5445 from General Farrell to O’Leary [Groves assistant], August 9, 1945, Top Secret</strong></a></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-date field--type-datetime field--label-hidden field__item"><time datetime="1945-08-09T12:00:00Z" class="datetime">Aug 9, 1945</time>
</div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-source field--type-text-long field--label-inline">
    <div class="field__label">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p>RG 77, Tinian Files, April-December 1945, box 20, Envelope G Tinian Files, Top Secret</p>
</div>
          </div>>
    <div class="field__label">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p>RG 77, Tinian Files, April-December 1945, box 20, Envelope G Tinian Files, Top Secret</p>
</div>
          </div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-description field--type-text-with-summary field--label-hidden field__item">
<p>The prime target for the second atomic attack was Kokura, which had a large army arsenal and ordnance works, but various problems ruled that city out; instead, the crew of the B-29 that carried “Fat Man” flew to an alternate target at Nagasaki. These cables are the earliest reports of the mission; the bombing of Nagasaki killed immediately at least 39,000 people, with more dying later. According to Frank, the “actual total of deaths due to the atomic bombs will never be known,” but the “huge number” ranges somewhere between 100,000 and 200,000 people. Barton J. Bernstein and Martin Sherwin have argued that if top Washington policymakers had kept tight control of the delivery of the bomb instead of delegating it to Groves the attack on Nagasaki could have been avoided. The combination of the first bomb and the Soviet declaration of war would have been enough to induce Tokyo’s surrender. By contrast, Maddox argues that Nagasaki was necessary so that Japanese “hardliners” could not “minimize the first explosion” or otherwise explain it away.<a name="_ednref61"></a><a href="#_edn61">[61]</a></p>
</div>

</div>

</div>
  </article>
</div>

</div>

</div><img loading="lazy" src="/sites/default/files/styles/thumbnail/public/documents/rdl56b-89y7n/72b.pdf.jpeg?itok=Te5kM9oH" alt="72b" typeof="foaf:Image" class="image-style-thumbnail" />

</div>

  <div class="entity-list">
    <h3 id="documents-73a-b-ramsey-letter-from-tinian-island" class="entity-list-title"><p><b>Documents 73A-B: Ramsey Letter from Tinian Island</b></p></h3>

<div class="field field--name-field-documents field--type-entity-reference field--label-hidden field__items">
              <div class="field__item"><article class="media media--type-document media--view-mode-document-list">

<div class="row">
              <div class="col-auto thumbnail-column">eld__items">
              <div class="field__item"><article class="media media--type-document media--view-mode-document-list">

<div class="row">
              <div class="col-auto thumbnail-column">

<div class="field field--name-thumbnail field--type-image field--label-hidden field__item">  <img loading="lazy" src="/sites/default/files/styles/thumbnail/public/documents/rdl8z8-9sm3l/73a.pdf.jpeg?itok=vwY6YIMG" alt="73a" typeof="foaf:Image" class="image-style-thumbnail" />

</div>

</div>

<div class="col fields-column">

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-section field--type-text-long field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Document 73A</p>
</div>

<div class="field field--name-field-title field--type-string field--label-hidden field__item"><a href="/document/28538-document-73a-letter-norman-ramsey-j-robert-oppenheimer-undated-mid-august-1945" hreflang="en"><strong class=" wrapped-title raw-title">Letter from Norman Ramsey to J. Robert Oppenheimer, undated [mid-August 1945], Secret, excerpts</strong></a></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-date field--type-datetime field--label-hidden field__item"><time datetime="1945-08-15T12:00:00Z" class="datetime">Aug 15, 1945</time>
</div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-source field--type-text-long field--label-inline">
    <div class="field__label">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p>Library of Congress, J. Robert Oppenheimer Papers, box 60, Ramsey, Norman</p>
</div>
          </div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-description field--type-text-with-summary field--label-hidden field__item">
<p>Ramsey, a physicist, served as deputy director of the bomb delivery group, Project Alberta. This personal account, written on Tinian, reports his fears about the danger of a nuclear accident, the confusion surrounding the Nagasaki attack, and early Air Force thinking about a nuclear strike force.</p></div>

</div>written on Tinian, reports his fears about the danger of a nuclear accident, the confusion surrounding the Nagasaki attack, and early Air Force thinking about a nuclear strike force.</p></div>

</div>

</div>
  </article>
</div>
              <div class="field__item"><article class="media media--type-document media--view-mode-document-list">

  <div class="entity-list">
    <h3 id="xi-toward-surrender" class="entity-list-title"><a href="#_tcref10" name="_tc10">XI. Toward Surrender</a></h3>

<div class="field field--name-field-documents field--type-entity-reference field--label-hidden field__items">
              <div class="field__item"><article class="media media--type-document media--view-mode-document-list">

<div class="row">
              <div class="col-auto thumbnail-column">

<div class="field field--name-thumbnail field--type-image field--label-hidden field__item">  <img loading="lazy" src="/sites/default/files/styles/thumbnail/public/documents/rdl56b-89y7n/74.pdf.jpeg?itok=8DusiO-B" alt="74" typeof="foaf:Image" class="image-style-thumbnail" />

</div>

</div>

<div class="col fields-column">

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-section field--type-text-long field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Document 74</p></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-title field--type-string field--label-hidden field__item"><a href="/document/28430-document-74-magic-far-east-summary-war-department-office-assistant-chief-staff-g-2" hreflang="en"><strong class=" wrapped-title raw-title">“Magic” – Far East Summary, War Department, Office of Assistant Chief of Staff, G-2, no. 507, August 9, 1945</strong></a></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-date field--type-datetime field--label-hidden field__item"><time datetime="1945-08-09T12:00:00Z" class="datetime">Aug 9, 1945</time>
</div>1945</strong></a></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-date field--type-datetime field--label-hidden field__item"><time datetime="1945-08-09T12:00:00Z" class="datetime">Aug 9, 1945</time>
</div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-source field--type-text-long field--label-inline">
    <div class="field__label">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p>RG 457, Summaries of Intercepted Japanese Messages (“Magic” Far East Summary, March 20, 1942 – October 2, 1945), box 7, SRS 491-547</p>
</div>
          </div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-description field--type-text-with-summary field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Within days after the bombing of Hiroshima, U.S. military intelligence intercepted Japanese reports on the destruction of the city. According to an “Eyewitness Account (and Estimates Heard) … In Regard to the Bombing of Hiroshima”: “Casualties have been estimated at 100,000 persons.”</p>
</div>

</div>

</div>
  </article>
</div>
              <div class="field__item"><article class="media media--type-document media--view-mode-document-list">

<div class="row">
              <div class="col-auto thumbnail-column">

<div class="field field--name-thumbnail field--type-image field--label-hidden field__item">  <img loading="lazy" src="/sites/default/files/styles/thumbnail/public/documents/rdl56b-89y7n/75.pdf.jpeg?itok=0GG1Mm1l" alt="75" typeof="foaf:Image" class="image-style-thumbnail" />

</div>

</div>

<div class="col fields-column">

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-section field--type-text-long field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Document 75</p></div>/>

</div>

</div>

<div class="col fields-column">

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-section field--type-text-long field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Document 75</p></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-title field--type-string field--label-hidden field__item"><a href="/document/28431-document-75-hoshina-memorandum-emperors-sacred-decision-go-seidan-9-10-august-1945" hreflang="en"><strong class=" wrapped-title raw-title">“Hoshina Memorandum” on the Emperor’s “Sacred Decision [<em>go-seidan]</em>,” 9-10 August, 1945</strong></a></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-date field--type-datetime field--label-hidden field__item"><time datetime="1945-08-09T12:00:00Z" class="datetime">Aug 9, 1945</time>
</div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-source field--type-text-long field--label-inline">
    <div class="field__label">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p>Zenshiro Hoshina, Daitoa Senso Hishi: Hoshina Zenshiro Kaiso-roku [Secret History of the Greater East Asia War: Memoir of Zenshiro Hoshina] (Tokyo, Japan: Hara-Shobo, 1975), excerpts from Section 5, “The Emperor made go-seidan [= the sacred decision] – the decision to terminate the war,” 139-149 [translation by Hikaru Tajima]</p>
</div>
          </div>Hara-Shobo, 1975), excerpts from Section 5, “The Emperor made go-seidan [= the sacred decision] – the decision to terminate the war,” 139-149 [translation by Hikaru Tajima]</p>
</div>
          </div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-description field--type-text-with-summary field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Despite the bombing of Hiroshima, the Soviet declaration of war, and growing worry about domestic instability, the Japanese cabinet (whose decisions required unanimity) could not form a consensus to accept the Potsdam Declaration. Members of the Supreme War Council—“the Big Six”<a name="_ednref62"></a><a href="#_edn62">[62]</a>—wanted the reply to Potsdam to include at least four conditions (e.g., no occupation, voluntary disarmament); they were willing to fight to the finish. The peace party, however, deftly maneuvered to break the stalemate by persuading a reluctant emperor to intervene. According to Hasegawa, Hirohito had become convinced that the preservation of the monarchy was at stake. Late in the evening of 9 August, the emperor and his advisers met in the bomb shelter of the Imperial Palace.</p>awa, Hirohito had become convinced that the preservation of the monarchy was at stake. Late in the evening of 9 August, the emperor and his advisers met in the bomb shelter of the Imperial Palace.</p>

<p>Zenshiro Hoshina, a senior naval official, attended the conference and prepared a detailed account. With Prime Minister Suzuki presiding, each of the ministers had a chance to state their views directly to Hirohito. While Army Minister Anami tacitly threatened a coup (“civil war”), the emperor accepted the majority view that the reply to the Potsdam declaration should include only one condition not the four urged by “Big Six.” Nevertheless, the condition that Hirohito accepted was not the one that foreign minister Togo had brought to the conference. What was at stake was the definition of the <em>kokutai</em> (national policy). Togo’s proposal would have been generally consistent with a constitutional monarchy because it defined the<em> kokutai</em> narrowly as the emperor and the imperial household. What Hirohito accepted, however, was a proposal by the extreme nationalist Kiichiro Hiranuma which drew upon prevailing understandings of the <em>kokutai</em>: the “mythical notion” that the emperor was a living god. “This was the affirmation of the emperor’s theocratic powers, unencumbered by any law, based on Shinto gods in antiquity, and totally incompatible with a constitutional monarchy.” Thus, the Japanese response to the Potsdam declaration opposed “any demand which prejudices the prerogatives of his Majesty as a sovereign ruler.” This proved to be unacceptable to the Truman administration.<a name="_ednref63"></a><a href="#_edn63">[63]</a></p>
</div>

</div>

</div>
  </article>
</div>

</div>

<div class="field field--name-field-date field--type-datetime field--label-hidden field__item"><time datetime="1945-08-10T12:00:00Z" class="datetime">Aug 10, 1945</time>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-description field--type-text-with-summary field--label-hidden field__item"><p>More intercepted messages on the bombing of Hiroshima.</p>

<div class="col fields-column">

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-section field--type-text-long field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Document 77A</p>

This issue of the diplomatic summary also includes Togo’s account of his notification of the Soviet declaration of war, reports of Soviet military operations in the Far East, and intercepts of French diplomatic traffic.</p>

<div class="field field--name-field-title field--type-string field--label-hidden field__item"><a href="/document/28434-document-77b-translation-intercepted-japanese-messages-circa-10-august-10-1945-top" hreflang="en"><strong class=" wrapped-title raw-title">Translation of intercepted Japanese messages, circa 10 August 10, 1945, Top Secret Ultra</strong></a></div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-source field--type-text-long field--label-inline">

<div class="col fields-column">

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-section field--type-text-long field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Document 78</p></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-title field--type-string field--label-hidden field__item"><a href="/document/28435-document-78-diary-entry-friday-august-10-1945-henry-wallace-diary" hreflang="en"><strong class=" wrapped-title raw-title">Diary Entry, Friday, August 10, 1945, Henry Wallace Diary</strong></a></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-date field--type-datetime field--label-hidden field__item"><time datetime="1945-08-10T12:00:00Z" class="datetime">Aug 10, 1945</time>
</div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-source field--type-text-long field--label-inline">
    <div class="field__label">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p>Papers of Henry A. Wallace, Special Collections Department, University of Iowa Libraries, Iowa City, Iowa (copy courtesy of Special Collections Department)</p></div>
          </div>="field__item"><p>Papers of Henry A. Wallace, Special Collections Department, University of Iowa Libraries, Iowa City, Iowa (copy courtesy of Special Collections Department)</p></div>
          </div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-description field--type-text-with-summary field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Secretary of Commerce (and former Vice President) Henry Wallace provided a detailed report on the cabinet meeting where Truman and his advisers discussed the Japanese surrender offer, Russian moves into Manchuria, and public opinion on “hard” surrender terms. With Japan close to capitulation, Truman asserted presidential control over nuclear weapons and later ordered that atomic bombs were not to be used on Japan without his “express authority from the President.” [See Document 82] Barton J. Bernstein has suggested that Truman’s comment about “all those kids” showed his belated recognition that the bomb caused mass casualties and that the target was not purely a military one.<a href="#_edn64" name="_ednref64">[64]</a></p></div>

</div>

</div>
  </article>
</div>
              <div class="field__item"><article class="media media--type-document media--view-mode-document-list">

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-section field--type-text-long field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Document 79</p></div>/>

<div class="col fields-column">

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-section field--type-text-long field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Document 79</p></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-title field--type-string field--label-hidden field__item"><a href="/document/28436-document-79-entries-10-11-august-robert-p-meiklejohn-diary" hreflang="en"><strong class=" wrapped-title raw-title">Entries for 10-11 August, Robert P. Meiklejohn Diary</strong></a></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-date field--type-datetime field--label-hidden field__item"><time datetime="1945-08-10T12:00:00Z" class="datetime">Aug 10, 1945</time>
</div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-source field--type-text-long field--label-inline">
    <div class="field__label">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p>W.A. Harriman Papers, Library of Congress, box 211, Robert Pickens Meiklejohn World War II Diary At London and Moscow March 10, 1941-February 14, 1946, Volume II (Privately printed, 1980 [Printed from hand-written originals]) (Reproduced with permission)</p>
</div>
          </div>d War II Diary At London and Moscow March 10, 1941-February 14, 1946, Volume II (Privately printed, 1980 [Printed from hand-written originals]) (Reproduced with permission)</p>
</div>
          </div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-description field--type-text-with-summary field--label-hidden field__item"><p>In these entries, Meiklejohn discussed how he and others in the Moscow Embassy learned about the bombing of Nagasaki from the “OWI Bulletin.” Entries for 10 and 11 August cover discussion at the Embassy about the radio broadcast announcing that Japan would surrender as long the Emperor’s status was not affected. Harriman opined that “surrender is in the bag” because of the Potsdam Declaration’s provision that the Japanese could “choose their own form of government, which would probably include the Emperor.” Further, “the only alternative to the Emperor is Communism,” implying that an official role for the Emperor was necessary to preserve social stability and prevent social revolution.</p>
</div>

</div>

</div>
  </article>
</div>
              <div class="field__item"><article class="media media--type-document media--view-mode-document-list">

<div class="row">
              <div class="col-auto thumbnail-column">

<div class="field field--name-thumbnail field--type-image field--label-hidden field__item">  <img loading="lazy" src="/sites/default/files/styles/thumbnail/public/documents/rdl56b-89y7n/80.pdf.jpeg?itok=ECXszyGY" alt="80" typeof="foaf:Image" class="image-style-thumbnail" />

</div>

</div>

<div class="col fields-column">

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-section field--type-text-long field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Document 80</p></div>/>

</div>

</div>

<div class="col fields-column">

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-section field--type-text-long field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Document 80</p></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-title field--type-string field--label-hidden field__item"><a href="/document/28437-document-80-stimson-diary-entries-friday-and-saturday-august-10-and-11-1945" hreflang="en"><strong class=" wrapped-title raw-title">Stimson Diary Entries, Friday and Saturday, August 10 and 11, 1945</strong></a></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-date field--type-datetime field--label-hidden field__item"><time datetime="1945-08-10T12:00:00Z" class="datetime">Aug 10, 1945</time>
</div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-source field--type-text-long field--label-inline">
    <div class="field__label">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p>Henry Stimson Diary, Sterling Library, Yale University (microfilm at Library of Congress)</p>
</div>
          </div><div class="field__label">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p>Henry Stimson Diary, Sterling Library, Yale University (microfilm at Library of Congress)</p>
</div>
          </div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-description field--type-text-with-summary field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Stimson’s account of the events of 10 August focused on the debate over the reply to the Japanese note, especially the question of the Emperor’s status. <a href="http://www.ibiblio.org/pha/policy/1945/1945-08-11a.html">The U.S. reply</a>, drafted during the course of the day, did not explicitly reject the note but suggested that any notion about the “prerogatives” of the Emperor would be superceded by the concept that all Japanese would be “Subject to the Supreme Commander of the Allied Powers.” The language was ambiguous enough to enable Japanese readers, upon Hirohito’s urging, to believe that they could decide for themselves the Emperor’s future role. Stimson accepted the language believing that a speedy reply to the Japanese would allow the United States to “get the homeland into our hands before the Russians could put in any substantial claim to occupy and help rule it.” If the note had included specific provision for a constitutional monarchy, Hasegawa argues, it would have “taken the wind out of the sails” of the military faction and Japan might have surrendered several days earlier, on August 11 or 12 instead of August 14.<a name="_ednref65"></a><a href="#_edn65">[65]</a></p>
</div>

</div>

</div>
  </article>
</div>

<div class="field field--name-field-title field--type-string field--label-hidden field__item"><a href="/document/28438-document-81-entries-walter-brown-diary-10-11-august-1945" hreflang="en"><strong class=" wrapped-title raw-title">Entries from Walter Brown Diary, 10-11 August 1945</strong></a></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-date field--type-datetime field--label-hidden field__item"><time datetime="1945-08-10T12:00:00Z" class="datetime">Aug 10, 1945</time>
</div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-source field--type-text-long field--label-inline">
    <div class="field__label">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p>Clemson University Libraries, Special Collections, Clemson, SC; Mss 243, Walter Brown Papers, box 68, folder 13, “Transcript/Draft B</p>
</div>
          </div><div class="field__item"><p>Clemson University Libraries, Special Collections, Clemson, SC; Mss 243, Walter Brown Papers, box 68, folder 13, “Transcript/Draft B</p>
</div>
          </div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-description field--type-text-with-summary field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Brown recounted Byrnes’ debriefing of the 10 August White House meeting on the Japanese peace offer, an account which differed somewhat from that in the Stimson diary. According to what Byrnes told Brown, Truman, Stimson, and Leahy favored accepting the Japanese note, but Byrnes objected that the United States should “go [no] further than we were willing to go at Potsdam.” Stimson’s account of the meeting noted Byrnes’ concerns (“troubled and anxious”) about the Japanese note and implied that he (Stimson) favored accepting it, but did not picture the debate as starkly as Browns's did.</p>
</div>

</div>

</div>
  </article>
</div>
              <div class="field__item"><article class="media media--type-document media--view-mode-document-list">

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-section field--type-text-long field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Document 82</p>
</div>>

</div>

<div class="col fields-column">

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-section field--type-text-long field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Document 82</p>
</div>

<div class="field field--name-field-title field--type-string field--label-hidden field__item"><a href="/document/28439-document-82-general-l-r-groves-chief-staff-george-c-marshall-august-10-1945-top" hreflang="en"><strong class=" wrapped-title raw-title">General L. R. Groves to Chief of Staff George C. Marshall, August 10, 1945, Top Secret, with a hand-written note by General Marshall</strong></a></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-date field--type-datetime field--label-hidden field__item"><time datetime="1945-08-10T12:00:00Z" class="datetime">Aug 10, 1945</time>
</div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-source field--type-text-long field--label-inline">
    <div class="field__label">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p>National Archives<a href="https://s3.amazonaws.com/NARAprodstorage/lz/rediscovery/03525_2011_001_a.jpg"> Catalog</a></p></div>
          </div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-description field--type-text-with-summary field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Groves informed General Marshall that he was making plans for the use of a third atomic weapon sometime after 17 August, depending on the weather. With Truman having ordered a halt to the atomic bombings [See document 78], Marshall wrote on Grove's memo that the bomb was “not to be released over Japan without express authority from the President.”</p></div>

</div>

</div>
  </article>

<div class="col fields-column">

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-section field--type-text-long field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Document 83</p></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-title field--type-string field--label-hidden field__item"><a href="/document/28440-document-83-memorandum-conversation-japanese-surrender-negotiations-august-10-1945" hreflang="en"><strong class=" wrapped-title raw-title">Memorandum of Conversation, “Japanese Surrender Negotiations,” August 10, 1945, Top Secret</strong></a></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-date field--type-datetime field--label-hidden field__item"><time datetime="1945-08-10T12:00:00Z" class="datetime">Aug 10, 1945</time>
</div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-source field--type-text-long field--label-inline">
    <div class="field__label">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p>Library of Congress Manuscript Division, Papers of W. Averell Harriman, box 181, Chron File Aug 10-12, 1945</p>
</div>
          </div>d__label">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p>Library of Congress Manuscript Division, Papers of W. Averell Harriman, box 181, Chron File Aug 10-12, 1945</p>
</div>
          </div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-description field--type-text-with-summary field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Japan’s prospective surrender was the subject of detailed discussion between Harriman, British Ambassador Kerr, and Soviet Foreign Minister Molotov during the evening of August 10 (with a follow-up meeting occurring at 2 a.m.). In the course of the conversation, Harriman received a message from Washington that included the proposed U.S. reply and a request for Soviet support of the reply. After considerable pressure from Harriman, the Soviets signed off on the reply but not before tensions surfaced over the control of Japan--whether Moscow would have a Supreme Commander there as well. This marked the beginning of a U.S.-Soviet “tug of war” over occupation arrangements for Japan.<a name="_ednref66"></a><a href="#_edn66">[66]</a></p>
</div>

</div>

</div>
  </article>
</div>
              <div class="field__item"><article class="media media--type-document media--view-mode-document-list">

<div class="row">
              <div class="col-auto thumbnail-column">

<div class="field field--name-thumbnail field--type-image field--label-hidden field__item">  <img loading="lazy" src="/sites/default/files/styles/thumbnail/public/documents/rdl56b-89y7n/84.pdf.jpeg?itok=UiXXFj0Q" alt="84" typeof="foaf:Image" class="image-style-thumbnail" />

</div>

<div class="col fields-column">

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-section field--type-text-long field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Document 84</p></div>/>

</div>

</div>

<div class="col fields-column">

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-section field--type-text-long field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Document 84</p></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-title field--type-string field--label-hidden field__item"><a href="/document/28441-document-84-admiral-takagi-diary-entry-12-august-1945" hreflang="en"><strong class=" wrapped-title raw-title">Admiral Takagi Diary Entry for 12 August [1945]</strong></a></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-date field--type-datetime field--label-hidden field__item"><time datetime="1945-08-12T12:00:00Z" class="datetime">Aug 12, 1945</time>
</div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-source field--type-text-long field--label-inline">
    <div class="field__label">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p>Takashi Itoh, ed., Sokichi Takagi: Nikki to Joho [Sokichi Takagi: Diary and Documents] (Tokyo, Japan: Misuzu-Shobo, 2000), 926-927 [Translation by Hikaru Tajima]</p></div>
          </div>d__item"><p>Takashi Itoh, ed., Sokichi Takagi: Nikki to Joho [Sokichi Takagi: Diary and Documents] (Tokyo, Japan: Misuzu-Shobo, 2000), 926-927 [Translation by Hikaru Tajima]</p></div>
          </div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-description field--type-text-with-summary field--label-hidden field__item"><p>As various factions in the government maneuvered on how to respond to the Byrnes note, Navy Minister Yonai and Admiral Takagi discussed the latest developments. Yonai was upset that Chief of Staff Yoshijiro Umezu and naval chief Suemu Toyada had sent the emperor a memorandum arguing that acceptance of the Brynes note would “desecrate the emperor’s dignity” and turn Japan into virtually a “slave nation.” The emperor chided Umezu and Toyoda for drawing hasty conclusions; in this he had the support of Yonai, who also dressed them down. As Yonai explained to Takagi, he had also confronted Naval Vice Chief Takijiro Onishi to make sure that he obeyed any decision by the Emperor. Yonai made sure that Takagi understood his reasons for bringing the war to an end and why he believed that the atomic bomb and the Soviet declaration of war had made it easier for Japan to surrender.<a href="#_edn67" name="_ednref67">[67]</a></p></div>

</div>

</div>
  </article>
</div>
              <div class="field__item"><article class="media media--type-document media--view-mode-document-list">

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-section field--type-text-long field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Document 85</p></div>/>
